\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
. The seemingly redundant presence of
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
is justified by the term
(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}
in (
2.35
), which will be absorbed by the RHS of the transport equation for
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
.

2.

Forbidden terms at order
\lambda^{0}
.
In (
2.30
), the forbidden terms in
\cos\left(\frac{2u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)
can be set to zero by imposing a polarization condition for
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
. This is only possible if the forbidden term that need to be absorbed has the tensorial structure
\partial_{(\alpha}u_{\mathbf{A}}Q_{\beta)}
for some 1-form
Q
. Here, this is even better since
Q=\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{A}}
(recall (
2.16
))! If the polarization condition for
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
holds, this extra bit of structure would imply
(F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}}=0
(where we used (
1.7
)).

3.

Forbidden terms at order
\lambda^{1}
.
In (
2.35
), two types of forbidden terms occur. First, the term
(F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}
coming from the quasi-linear wave operator oscillates like
\cos\left(\frac{3u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)
. However, the previous comment precisely says that it vanishes thanks to the structure of the semi-linear forbidden terms that the polarization condition for
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
is absorbing. Second, the term
\partial_{(\alpha}u(\tilde{R}^{(1,h,T,u)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\beta)}
precisely has the required structure to be absorbed by a polarization condition for
\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T}
.

4.

Mixed harmonics at order
\lambda^{0}
.
The term
R^{(0)}_{\mathrm{mixed}}
given by (
2.31
) will be simply canceled by asking
F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
to solve

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}}\left(F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right)=\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}.

Since
\mathbf{g}_{0}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}),\mathrm{d}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}))\neq 0
(recall (
1.18
)), Lemma
1.1
shows that this equation is solvable if and only if
\mathscr{P}\left[\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}\right]=0
. We will check this in Section
2.3.1
but we draw the reader’s attention on the importance of this simple algebraic fact which is a reminder of Einstein vacuum equations’ transparency.

5.

Mixed harmonics at order
\lambda^{1}
.
The structure of
R^{(1)}_{\mathrm{mixed}}
differs from the one of
R^{(0)}_{\mathrm{mixed}}
since instead of having the operator
\mathcal{P}_{v}
acting on
\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T}
, we only have the part of
\mathcal{P}_{v}
coming from the wave part of the Ricci tensor, i.e
-\mathbf{g}_{0}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}v)\mathrm{Id}
. Opposite to
\mathcal{P}_{v}
, this operator has full range and no condition on the RHS needs to hold, which simplifies greatly our construction since we don’t need the exact expression of
\tilde{R}^{(1,e,T,v)}_{\mathbf{A}}
. The absence of the other half of
\mathcal{P}_{v}
is due to our choice of generalised wave gauge, i.e our choice of
\Upsilon^{\rho}
(recall the second comment after Lemma
2.3
). We see here how the gauge helps us recover true ellipticity when considering mixed harmonics, as it usually does help us recover true hyperbolicity when solving (
1.1
).

2.3
The hierarchy

In this section, following the general mechanisms presented after Proposition
2.1
, we derive a hierarchy of equations and relations for the various terms in
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}
which will ensure
R_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda})=0
by ensuring
R^{(0)}_{\alpha\beta}=0
,
R^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}=0
and
R^{(\geq 2)}_{\alpha\beta}=0
.

2.3.1
Ensuring
R^{(0)}_{\alpha\beta}=0

Looking at (
2.29
), we first want the non-oscillating terms to vanish. For that, we impose the following backreaction condition for
F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
:

\displaystyle\left|F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right|^{2}_{\mathbf{g}_{0}}

\displaystyle=8F_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}.

We wish to have
R^{(0)}_{\mathrm{null}}=0
. Looking at (
2.30
), we cancel the admissible harmonics by imposing the following transport equation for
F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=0,

as well as a polarization condition for
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\left[\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\right]

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\left[F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\right]

\displaystyle=-Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}},

where
Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}}
is defined in (
2.32
) (this is not clear that (
2.32
) actually defines a tensor, see Remark
2.2
for a justification of this fact). We cancel the forbidden harmonics in
R^{(0)}_{\mathrm{null}}
by imposing a polarization condition for
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\left[F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{32}\left|F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right|^{2}_{\mathbf{g}_{0}}\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{A}}.

For convenience, we define the following tensors

\displaystyle V^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathscr{P}\left[F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\right]+Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}},

(2.43)

\displaystyle V^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathscr{P}\left[F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\right]+\frac{3}{32}\left|F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right|^{2}_{\mathbf{g}_{0}}\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{A}},

(2.44)

so that the polarization conditions for
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
rewrite

\displaystyle V^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=0,

(2.45)

\displaystyle V^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=0.

(2.46)

Remark 2.2
.

Note that
Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}}
defined by (
2.32
) is actually a tensor. More generally, one can check that

\displaystyle S^{\mu\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}T_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}T_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=S^{\mu\nu}\left(\mathbf{D}_{\mu}T_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}T_{\mu\nu}+\Gamma(\mathbf{g})^{\rho}_{\mu\nu}T_{\sigma\rho}\right)

so that

\displaystyle S^{\mu\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}T_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}T_{\mu\nu}\right)

is the
\partial_{\sigma}
component of a 1-form. In particular,
V^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
V^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
defined in (
2.43
) and (
2.44
) are tensors so that the polarization conditions (
2.45
) and (
2.46
) are not gauge conditions.

We cancel the mixed term
R^{(0)}_{\mathrm{mixed}}
given by (
2.31
) by imposing the following equation for
F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}}\left(F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

(2.47)

where
\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
is defined by (
2.33
). Thanks to Lemma
1.1
, this equation is solvable if and only its RHS satisfies some polarization condition.

Lemma 2.4
.

We assume that (
2.6
) holds. If we define

\displaystyle F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-\frac{\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}}{\mathbf{g}_{0}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}),\mathrm{d}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}))},

then (
2.47
) holds.

Proof.

According to Lemma
1.1
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\left[\mathcal{I}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}^{(0,\pm)}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}\right]=0.

(2.48)

For clarity we define

\displaystyle A_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-\frac{1}{4}\left((F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}+(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{B}}L_{\mathbf{B}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})_{\alpha\beta}\right),

\displaystyle B_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\partial_{(\alpha}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})\bigg(-\frac{1}{8}\partial_{\beta)}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})\left|F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}}\right|_{\mathbf{g}_{0}}-\frac{1}{4}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})^{\nu}_{L_{\mathbf{A}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\nu\beta)}\mp\frac{1}{4}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})^{\nu}_{L_{\mathbf{B}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})_{\nu\beta)}\bigg).

so that
(\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{\alpha\beta}=A_{\alpha\beta}+B_{\alpha\beta}+(P^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{\alpha\beta}
. Thanks to (
2.6
) we first have

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\left[A\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}\right]_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left(\pm(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha L_{\mathbf{B}}}+(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{B}}L_{\mathbf{B}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})_{\alpha L_{\mathbf{A}}}\right).

For
B_{\alpha\beta}
, (
1.10
) gives

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\left[B\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}\right]_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\mp\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{g}_{0}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{A}},\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{B}})\bigg((F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})^{\nu}_{L_{\mathbf{A}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\nu}\pm(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})^{\nu}_{L_{\mathbf{B}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})_{\alpha\nu}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\frac{1}{2}\left|F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}}\right|_{g_{0}}\partial_{\alpha}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})\bigg).

For
(P^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{\alpha\beta}
, (
2.17
) and (
2.6
) give

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\left[P^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}\right]_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=-(P^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{\alpha(L_{\mathbf{A}}\pm L_{\mathbf{B}})}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbf{g}_{0}}P^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}((L_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha}\pm(L_{\mathbf{B}})_{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{1}{4}\partial_{\alpha}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})\mathbf{g}_{0}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{A}},\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{B}})\left|F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}}\right|_{\mathbf{g}_{0}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{g}_{0}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{A}},\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{B}})\left((F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{B}}\sigma}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})_{\alpha}^{\sigma}\pm(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}\sigma}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha}^{\sigma}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{4}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{B}}L_{\mathbf{B}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})_{\alpha L_{\mathbf{A}}}\mp\frac{1}{4}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha L_{\mathbf{B}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}}

\displaystyle=-\mathscr{P}\left[A\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}\right]_{\alpha}-\mathscr{P}\left[B\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}\right]_{\alpha},

which concludes the proof of (
2.48
) and thus of the lemma.
∎

Remark 2.3
.

If one is interested in the strict equivalent to Choquet-Bruhat’s singlephase approximate construction from
[
CB69
]
in the multiphase case, the above conditions are sufficient since they indeed lead to
R_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda})=O\left(\lambda\right)
.

2.3.2
Ensuring
R^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}=0

Looking at (
2.34
), we wish to have
R^{(1)}_{\mathrm{null}}=0
and
R^{(1)}_{\mathrm{mixed}}=0
. We first consider
R^{(1)}_{\mathrm{null}}
and rewrite it with the help of the tensors
V^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
V^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
defined by (
2.43
) and (
2.44
). Thanks to (
2.35
) we obtain

\displaystyle 2(R^{(1)}_{\mathrm{null}})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\cos\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\bigg((\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}+(\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}+(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}+(\tilde{R}^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}

(2.49)

\displaystyle-\mathbf{D}_{(\alpha}\mathscr{P}\left[\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\right]_{\beta)}-\mathbf{D}_{(\alpha}(V^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\beta)}+\mathbf{D}_{(\alpha}(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\beta)}\bigg)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\sin\left(\frac{2u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\bigg((\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{D}_{(\alpha}(V^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\beta)}

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{32}\mathbf{D}_{(\alpha}\left(\left|F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right|^{2}_{\mathbf{g}_{0}}\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{A}}\right)_{\beta)}+(\tilde{R}^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}\bigg)

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\bigg(2\sin\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\sin\left(\frac{2u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)-\cos\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\cos\left(\frac{2u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\bigg)(V^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\sin\left(\frac{2u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)(\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle+\sum_{u\in\mathcal{N},T\in\{\cos,\sin\}}T\left(\frac{u}{\lambda}\right)\partial_{(\alpha}u\left(-\mathscr{P}\left[\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T}\middle|u\right]_{\beta)}+(\tilde{R}^{(1,h,T,u)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\beta)}\right).

Since the above polarization conditions would imply
V^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}=V^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}=\mathscr{P}\left[\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\right]=0
, we cancel the admissible harmonics in
(R^{(1)}_{\mathrm{null}})_{\alpha\beta}
by imposing transport equations for
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
:

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{D}_{(\alpha}(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\beta)}-(\tilde{R}^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta},

(2.50)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{32}\mathbf{D}_{(\alpha}\left(\left|F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right|^{2}_{\mathbf{g}_{0}}\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{A}}\right)_{\beta)}-(\tilde{R}^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta},

(2.51)

The equation for
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
needs to be adjusted so that the coupling with the equation for
\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}
leads to a well-posed system in the high-frequency limit
\lambda\to 0
. As in
[
Tou23a
]
we impose the following transport equation

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=-\Pi_{\leq}\left((\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}}\right)F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}},

(2.52)

where the operator
\Pi_{\leq}
is defined by

\displaystyle\Pi_{\leq}(f)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\chi_{\lambda}\mathcal{F}(f)\right),

where
\chi_{\lambda}(\xi)=\chi(\lambda\xi)
for
\chi:\mathbb{R}^{3}\longrightarrow[0,1]
a smooth function supported in
\{|\xi|\leq 2\}
and such that
\chi_{|_{\{|\xi|\leq 1\}}}=1
and where
\mathcal{F}
is the standard Fourier transform on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. In order to cancel the non-tangential terms containing forbidden harmonics we impose a polarization condition for
\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T}

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\left[\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T}\middle|u\right]

\displaystyle=\tilde{R}^{(1,h,T,u)}_{\mathbf{A}}.

(2.53)

In order to cancel
R^{(1)}_{\mathrm{mixed}}
given by (
2.36
), we simply define
\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T}
by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\mathbf{g}_{0}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}v)}\tilde{R}^{(1,e,T,v)}_{\mathbf{A}},

(2.54)

where we recall that if
v\in\mathcal{I}
, then
\mathbf{g}_{0}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}v)\neq 0
(see Section
1.5
).

2.3.3
Ensuring
R^{(\geq 2)}_{\alpha\beta}=0

Thanks to its expression given by (
2.41
), in order to cancel
R^{(\geq 2)}_{\alpha\beta}
it suffices to impose a wave equation for the remainder
\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}
. This equation needs to be adjusted to handle the coupling with the equation for
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
, and we also need to set aside the problematic derivatives all contained in
\Upsilon^{\rho}
. Therefore we impose the following wave equation for
\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle\tilde{\Box}_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\sin\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\tilde{\Box}_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}-\lambda\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathcal{N}\\
T\in\{\cos,\sin\}\end{subarray}}T\left(\frac{u}{\lambda}\right)\tilde{\Box}_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathcal{I}\\
T\in\{\cos,\sin\}\end{subarray}}T\left(\frac{v}{\lambda}\right)\tilde{\Box}_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T})_{\alpha\beta}+\tilde{R}^{(2)}_{\alpha\beta}+\left(\mathring{H}^{\rho}\partial_{\rho}(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda})_{\alpha\beta}+(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda})_{\rho(\alpha}\partial_{\beta)}\mathring{H}^{\rho}\right)^{(\geq 2)}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\cos\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\Pi_{\geq}\left((\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}}\right)(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta},

where the operator
\Pi_{\geq}
is defined by
\Pi_{\geq}=\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\leq}
. We also impose the following generalised wave gauge condition

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{\rho}

\displaystyle=0.

2.4
Reformulation of Theorem
1.3

The conclusion of Sections
2.3.1
,
2.3.2
and
2.3.3
is the following: if the tensors
F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}
solve the system

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=0,

(2.55)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{D}_{(\alpha}(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\beta)}-(\tilde{R}^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta},

(2.56)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{32}\mathbf{D}_{(\alpha}\left(\left|F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right|^{2}_{\mathbf{g}_{0}}\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{A}}\right)_{\beta)}-(\tilde{R}^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta},

(2.57)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=-\Pi_{\leq}\left((\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}}\right)F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}},

(2.58)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Box}_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\sin\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\tilde{\Box}_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta}-\lambda\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathcal{N}\\
T\in\{\cos,\sin\}\end{subarray}}T\left(\frac{u}{\lambda}\right)\tilde{\Box}_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T})_{\alpha\beta}

(2.59)

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathcal{I}\\
T\in\{\cos,\sin\}\end{subarray}}T\left(\frac{v}{\lambda}\right)\tilde{\Box}_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T})_{\alpha\beta}+\tilde{R}^{(2)}_{\alpha\beta}+\left(\mathring{H}^{\rho}\partial_{\rho}(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda})_{\alpha\beta}+(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda})_{\rho(\alpha}\partial_{\beta)}\mathring{H}^{\rho}\right)^{(\geq 2)}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\cos\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\Pi_{\geq}\left((\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}}\right)(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta},

together with the polarization conditions

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\left[F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\right]

\displaystyle=0,

(2.60)

\displaystyle F^{(1)}_{L_{\mathbf{A}}\underline{L}_{\mathbf{A}}}

\displaystyle=0,

(2.61)

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\left[\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\right]

\displaystyle=0,

(2.62)

\displaystyle V^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=0,

(2.63)

\displaystyle V^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=0,

(2.64)

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\left[\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T}\middle|u\right]

\displaystyle=\tilde{R}^{(1,h,T,u)}_{\mathbf{A}},

(2.65)

the backreaction condition

\displaystyle\left|F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right|^{2}_{\mathbf{g}_{0}}=8F_{\mathbf{A}}^{2},

(2.66)

the generalised wave gauge condition

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{\rho}

\displaystyle=0,

(2.67)

with
F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T}
defined by

\displaystyle(F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-\frac{(\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{\alpha\beta}}{\mathbf{g}_{0}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}),\mathrm{d}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}))},

(2.68)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-\frac{1}{\mathbf{g}_{0}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}v)}\tilde{R}^{(1,e,T,v)}_{\mathbf{A}},

(2.69)

then the metric
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}
defined by (
2.1
) solves the Einstein vacuum equations (
1.1
). The initial data for the transport-wave system (
2.55
)-(
2.59
) consist in initial values on
\Sigma_{0}
for

\displaystyle(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta},\quad(F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta},\quad(F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta},\quad(\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\alpha\beta},\quad(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{\alpha\beta}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{\alpha\beta},

where
\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}
is the future-directed unit normal to
\Sigma_{0}
for
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}
(which can be computed from
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda|_{\Sigma_{0}}}
). Therefore, the task of proving Theorem
1.3
is divided into three steps:

1.

In Section
3
, we construct initial data such that the algebraic conditions (
2.60
)-(
2.67
) and the constraint equations are satisfied on
\Sigma_{0}
.

2.

In Section
4
, we solve the system (
2.55
)-(
2.59
) on
[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

3.

In Section
5
, we show that the algebraic conditions (
2.60
)-(
2.67
) actually hold on the full spacetime
[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Remark 2.4
.

Thanks to (
2.68
), (
2.33
) and (
2.17
) we have
F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\left\{(F^{(1)})^{2}\right\}
. This would be used without mention in the rest of this article.

Remark 2.5
.

The tensors
\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T}
only need to satisfy the polarization condition
2.65
and in particular are not obtained by solving partial differential equations. Therefore, we can simply define
\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T}
for
u=ku_{\mathbf{A}}
by setting

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-\frac{1}{k}(\tilde{R}^{(1,h,T,u)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}},

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-\frac{1}{k}(\tilde{R}^{(1,h,T,u)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}},

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T})_{e^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}e^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-\frac{1}{k}(\tilde{R}^{(1,h,T,u)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\underline{L}_{\mathbf{A}}},

and by setting all the other components of
\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T}
in the frame
\left(L_{\mathbf{A}},\underline{L}_{\mathbf{A}},e^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}},e^{(2)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)
to 0. In particular, thanks to (
2.39
) this implies

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\partial F^{(2,1)}+\partial F^{(2,2)}+\left(\mathfrak{F}+F^{(2,1)}+F^{(2,2)}+(F^{(1)})^{2}+\partial F^{(1)}\right)\left(1+F^{(1)}\right)\Big\},

(2.70)

where we already applied Remark
2.4
and replaced
F^{(2,\pm)}
by
(F^{(1)})^{2}
in the schematic notation.

3
Construction of initial data

In this section, we construct initial data for the system (
2.55
)-(
2.59
) on
\Sigma_{0}
. They need to be such that the algebraic conditions (
2.60
)-(
2.67
) hold on
\Sigma_{0}
and also such that the constraint equations are satisfied by the induced metric on
\Sigma_{0}
and the second fundamental form of
\Sigma_{0}
. These equations are

\displaystyle R(g)-|k|^{2}_{g}+(\mathrm{tr}_{g}k)^{2}

\displaystyle=0,

(3.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{g}k-\mathrm{d}\mathrm{tr}_{g}k

\displaystyle=0,

where
R(g)
is the scalar curvature of
g
.

3.1
From a seed to solutions to the constraint equations

The construction of initial data is based on a seed, with which we first construct a family of solutions to the constraint equations and then pick a particular element of this family to define appropriate initial data for the system (
2.55
)-(
2.59
).

Definition 3.1
.

A seed is a family
\left(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)_{\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{A}}
of symmetric 2-tensors on
\Sigma_{0}
such that for all
\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{A}
we have

\displaystyle\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}=F_{\mathbf{A}}\left(\theta^{+}_{\mathbf{A}}\left(e^{(1),\flat}_{\mathbf{A}}\otimes e^{(1),\flat}_{\mathbf{A}}-e^{(2),\flat}_{\mathbf{A}}\otimes e^{(2),\flat}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)+\theta^{\times}_{\mathbf{A}}\left(e^{(1),\flat}_{\mathbf{A}}\otimes e^{(2),\flat}_{\mathbf{A}}+e^{(2),\flat}_{\mathbf{A}}\otimes e^{(1),\flat}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)\right),

(3.2)

where
e^{(\mathbf{i}),\flat}_{\mathbf{A}}
denotes the 1-form canonically associated to the vector field
e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}
by
g_{0}
and where
\left(\theta^{+}_{\mathbf{A}},\theta^{\times}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)
are numbers satisfying
\left(\theta^{+}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)^{2}+\left(\theta^{\times}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)^{2}=4
.

The expression (
3.2
) directly implies that
\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=0,

(3.3)

\displaystyle(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}i}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\left|\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right|^{2}_{g_{0}}

\displaystyle=8F_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}.

Moreover, the assumptions made on
F_{\mathbf{A}}
in Section
1.5
imply that
\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
is supported in
B_{R}
and satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{H^{N}(\Sigma_{0})}\lesssim\varepsilon.

(3.4)

Remark 3.1
.

Note the analogy between (
3.2
) and the TT gauge of linearized gravity, where
\theta^{+}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
\theta^{\times}_{\mathbf{A}}
would play the role as the coefficients of the two possible polarizations for the gravitational wave.

Before stating our main result on the constraint equations, we introduce two operators acting on the space of symmetric 2-tensors on
\Sigma_{0}
:

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[1]}_{v}(S)

\displaystyle=S-\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}S-S_{N_{v}N_{v}}\right)g_{0},

(3.5)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}_{v}(S)_{ij}

\displaystyle=S_{ij}+(N_{v})_{(i}\left((N_{v})_{j)}\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}S-S_{N_{v}j)}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}S-S_{N_{v}N_{v}}\right)(g_{0})_{ij},

(3.6)

where
v
is a scalar function on
\Sigma_{0}
such that
|\nabla_{g_{0}}v|_{g_{0}}\neq 0
and

\displaystyle N_{v}\vcentcolon=\frac{\nabla_{g_{0}}v}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}v|_{g_{0}}}.

Note that
N_{\mathbf{A}}=N_{u_{\mathbf{A}}}
and
N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=N_{u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}}
where
N_{\mathbf{A}}
and
N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
are defined in (
1.25
). Next lemma gives important properties of these operators which will be used in Section
3.2
, its proof is left to the reader.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
v
a scalar function on
\Sigma_{0}
such that
|\nabla_{g_{0}}v|_{g_{0}}\neq 0
. We have
\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[1]}_{v}\circ\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[1]}_{v}=\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[1]}_{v}
and
\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}_{v}\circ\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}_{v}=\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}_{v}
. Moreover we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{ran}\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[1]}_{v}

\displaystyle=\left\{S\text{ symmetric 2-tensors on $\Sigma_{0}$}~\middle|~\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}S=S_{N_{v}N_{v}}\right\},

(3.7)

\displaystyle\mathrm{ran}\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}_{v}

\displaystyle=\left\{S\text{ symmetric 2-tensors on $\Sigma_{0}$}~\middle|~(\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}S)(N_{v})_{i}=S_{iN_{v}}\right\}.

(3.8)

Finally, if
X
and
N
are two vector fields on
\Sigma_{0}
we define

\displaystyle(N\tilde{\otimes}X)_{ij}\vcentcolon=N_{(i}X_{j)}-\frac{1}{2}X_{N}(g_{0})_{ij},

(3.9)

where
X_{N}=g_{0}(X,N)
. We are now ready to state our main result on the constraint equations.

Proposition 3.1
.

Let
\left(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)_{\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{A}}
be a seed. Let
\left(\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}},\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)_{\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{A}}
and
\left(\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right)_{\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}\in\mathcal{A},\mathbf{A}\neq\mathbf{B}}
be families of symmetric 2-tensors on
\Sigma_{0}
supported in
B_{R}
with the symmetry property
\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}}
and
\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}}
and such that

\displaystyle\left\|\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right\|_{H^{N}(\Sigma_{0})}+\left\|\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{H^{N-1}(\Sigma_{0})}+\left\|\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{H^{N-1}(\Sigma_{0})}+\left\|\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right\|_{H^{N-1}(\Sigma_{0})}\leq\varepsilon,

(3.10)

If
\varepsilon
is small enough, there exists
(g_{\lambda},k_{\lambda})
a solution of the constraint equations on
\Sigma_{0}
of the form

\displaystyle g_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=g_{0}+\lambda\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\cos\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}+\lambda^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\sin\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)4\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}g_{0}+\lambda^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\cos\left(\frac{2u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\frac{3}{4}F_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}g_{0}

(3.11)

\displaystyle+\lambda^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{A}\neq\mathbf{B},\pm}\cos\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}}{\lambda}\right)\left(\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[1]}_{u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}}\left(\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right)+4\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}g_{0}\right)+\lambda^{2}h_{\lambda}

and

\displaystyle k_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=k_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\sin\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

(3.12)

\displaystyle+\lambda\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\cos\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\left(\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}_{u_{\mathbf{A}}}\left(\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)+\frac{N_{\mathbf{A}}\tilde{\otimes}\widecheck{X}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}\right)

\displaystyle+\lambda\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\sin\left(\frac{2u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\left(\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}_{u_{\mathbf{A}}}\left(\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)-\frac{3}{2}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}F_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}N_{\mathbf{A}}\tilde{\otimes}N_{\mathbf{A}}\right)

\displaystyle+\lambda\sum_{\mathbf{A}\neq\mathbf{B},\pm}\sin\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}}{\lambda}\right)\left(\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}_{u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}}\left(\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right)-\frac{N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\otimes}\widecheck{X}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}\left(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}\right)|_{g_{0}}}\right)+\lambda^{2}\tilde{k}^{cons}_{\lambda},

where
\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
,
\widecheck{X}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
\widecheck{X}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
are defined by

\displaystyle\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{8|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}\left(-\partial^{i}\partial^{j}u_{\mathbf{A}}+|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}k_{0}^{ij}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\ell}u_{\mathbf{A}}\partial^{\ell}g_{0}^{ij}\right),

(3.13)

\displaystyle\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\frac{\left|\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}}\right|_{g_{0}}}{64|\nabla_{g_{0}}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})|_{g_{0}}^{2}}\Big(2|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}+2|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}

(3.14)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\pm 3|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}\mp|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\mp\frac{(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{i\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})_{\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}}^{i}}{32|\nabla_{g_{0}}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})|_{g_{0}}^{2}},

and

\displaystyle(\widecheck{X}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{i}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{2}\partial^{b}\left(|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)_{bi}+\frac{1}{4}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{bc}\partial_{i}g_{0}^{bc}

(3.15)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\left(\partial_{a}g_{0}^{ca}+\frac{1}{2}g_{0}^{ab}g_{0}^{cd}\partial_{d}(g_{0})_{ab}\right)(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ic}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{i}u_{\mathbf{A}}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{bc}(k_{0})^{bc},

\displaystyle(\widecheck{X}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{i}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-\frac{1}{8}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})^{b}_{\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})_{bi}-\frac{1}{8}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})^{b}_{\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{bi}

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(\frac{1}{8}\left(|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\partial_{i}u_{\mathbf{A}}+|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}\partial_{i}u_{\mathbf{B}}\right)

(3.16)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{1}{16}\left(\pm|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}\partial_{i}u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\partial_{i}u_{\mathbf{B}}\right)\bigg)\left|\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}}\right|_{g_{0}}.

Moreover, the remainders
h_{\lambda}
and
\tilde{k}^{cons}_{\lambda}
belong to the spaces
H^{5}_{\delta}(\Sigma_{0})
and
H^{4}_{\delta+1}(\Sigma_{0})
respectively and satisfy

\displaystyle\max_{r\in\llbracket 0,4\rrbracket}\lambda^{r}\left\|\nabla^{r+1}h_{\lambda}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+r+1}(\Sigma_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{cons}}\varepsilon,

(3.17)

\displaystyle\max_{r\in\llbracket 0,4\rrbracket}\lambda^{r}\left\|\nabla^{r}\tilde{k}^{cons}_{\lambda}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+r+1}(\Sigma_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{cons}}\varepsilon,

(3.18)

for some
C_{\mathrm{cons}}=C_{\mathrm{cons}}(N,\delta,R)>0
.

The result of Proposition
3.1
will be used as a black box in the final construction of initial data in Proposition
3.2
, and its proof, based on the conformal method, is postponed to Appendix
B
. The freedom in the choice of the tensors
\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
,
\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
will be crucially used to match the solution of the constraint equations and the data induced by the spacetime metric. This is related to an inherent redundancy in the geometric optics approach for
1.1
. Indeed, solving the constraint roughly provides the zeroth and first derivatives of the metric on
\Sigma_{0}
. However, our geometric optics construction implies that the first terms in the ansatz for
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}
actually solve first order transport equation, so that their first order derivatives are not free. This difficulty was already present in the singlephase case of
[
Tou23a
,
Tou23b
]
, but the multiphase case brings a new one: the tensor
F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
solves an algebraic equation and is defined by (
2.68
) so that even its zeroth derivatives is not free. Therefore, we need to make sure that the solution
(g_{\lambda},k_{\lambda})
of the constraint equations matches the expression of the spacetime ansatz by choosing appropriate tensors
\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
,
\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
.

3.2
Initial data for the spacetime metric
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}

In this section, we define initial data for the system (
2.55
)-(
2.59
). The properties of these initial data are summarized in Proposition
3.2
at the end of the section.

3.2.1
Initial data for the metric and the induced metric

We start by defining initial data for the induced metric
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda|_{\Sigma_{0}}}
. In order to ensure that the induced metric on

is given by

from Proposition

Initial data for

.

For

\displaystyle(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij},

(3.19)

\displaystyle(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{0\alpha|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=0.

(3.20)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=N_{\mathbf{A}}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}+(\partial_{t}-N_{\mathbf{A}})^{\rho}\Gamma(\mathbf{g}_{0})^{k}_{\rho(i}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{kj)}+\frac{1}{2|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}(\Box_{\mathbf{g}_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}})(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij},

\displaystyle g_{0}^{ij}\partial_{t}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

(3.25)

\displaystyle N_{\mathbf{A}}^{i}N_{\mathbf{A}}^{j}\partial_{t}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=0,

(3.26)

\displaystyle N_{\mathbf{A}}^{i}(e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}})^{j}\partial_{t}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\left(-N_{\mathbf{A}}(N_{\mathbf{A}})_{\ell}-(k_{0})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}\ell}+\frac{1}{2}N_{\mathbf{A}}^{i}N_{\mathbf{A}}^{j}\partial_{\ell}(g_{0})_{ji}\right)g_{0}^{k\ell}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ke^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}}.

(3.28)

\displaystyle(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{0|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}k_{0}^{ij}.

(3.29)

Lemma 3.2
.

The initial data (
3.31
)-(
3.38
) are such that (
2.63
)-(
2.64
) hold on
\Sigma_{0}
.

Proof.

We recall that
L_{\mathbf{A}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}=|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}(\partial_{t}-N_{\mathbf{A}})
and
\underline{L}_{\mathbf{A}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}=|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}(\partial_{t}+N_{\mathbf{A}})
. We start with (
2.63
). From (

) and (

) we have

\displaystyle(V^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{P}\left[F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\right]_{L_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}+(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=-(F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}+(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}\left(-(F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{00|_{\Sigma_{0}}}+2(F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{0N_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}-(F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}N_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}\right)+(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}\left(4\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}-\frac{1}{|\nabla u_{\mathbf{A}}|^{2}}(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{{L_{\mathbf{A}}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}-(F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}N_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}\right)+(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=0,

and from (
1.8
) we also have

\displaystyle(V^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{P}\left[F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\right]_{e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}+(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=-(F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}+(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=-|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\left((F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}0|_{\Sigma_{0}}}-(F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}N_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}\right)+(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=-|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{{e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}-(F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}N_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}\right)+(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=0,

where we used (
3.31
) and (
3.32
)-(
3.34
). From (
1.9
) we also have

\displaystyle(V^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\underline{L}_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{P}\left[F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\right]_{\underline{L}_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}+(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\underline{L}_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}\left((F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}N_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}-\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}\;\;|_{\Sigma_{0}}}\right)+|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\left((Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{0|_{\Sigma_{0}}}+N_{\mathbf{A}}^{\ell}(Q^{(0)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\ell|_{\Sigma_{0}}}\right).

Using (
3.31
) together with (

) on one hand and (

)-(

\displaystyle(V^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\underline{L}_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=-8|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}+|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\left((\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}k_{0}^{ij}-\frac{(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\ell j}}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}g_{0}^{ij}\partial_{i}\partial^{\ell}u_{\mathbf{A}}+\frac{1}{2}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}N_{\mathbf{A}}g_{0}^{ij}\right)

\displaystyle=0,

where we used (
3.13
). We now turn to (
2.64
). From (
2.44
) we have

\displaystyle(V^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{P}\left[F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\right]_{L_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=-(F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}\left(-(F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{00|_{\Sigma_{0}}}+2(F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{0N_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}-(F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}N_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}\right)

\displaystyle=0,

and

\displaystyle(V^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

where we used (

) and (
3.36
)-(

). Using now only (

\displaystyle(V^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\underline{L}_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{P}\left[F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}\middle|u_{\mathbf{A}}\right]_{\underline{L}_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}+\frac{3}{2}F_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}|\nabla u_{\mathbf{A}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}\left((F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}N_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}-\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}\;\;|_{\Sigma_{0}}}\right)+\frac{3}{2}F_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle=0,

which concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Initial data for

.

)-(

) we want

\displaystyle=\left\{\nabla^{\leq 1}F^{(2,2)}+\left(F^{(2,1)}+\partial^{\leq 1}F^{(1)}\right)F^{(1)}\right\}_{\alpha\beta},

)-(

Therefore, using the various definitions of initial data above, this allows us to completely define the initial value of
(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T})_{\alpha\beta|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=(h_{\lambda})_{ij}-\lambda\sum_{u\in\mathcal{N},T\in\{\cos,\sin\}}T\left(\frac{u}{\lambda}\right)(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}-\lambda\sum_{v\in\mathcal{I},T\in\{\cos,\sin\}}T\left(\frac{v}{\lambda}\right)(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}},

(3.39)

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{0\alpha|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-\lambda\sum_{u\in\mathcal{N},T\in\{\cos,\sin\}}T\left(\frac{u}{\lambda}\right)(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T})_{0\alpha|_{\Sigma_{0}}}-\lambda\sum_{v\in\mathcal{I},T\in\{\cos,\sin\}}T\left(\frac{v}{\lambda}\right)(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T})_{0\alpha|_{\Sigma_{0}}}.

The induced metric.

Thanks to (
3.19
), (

), (
3.31
), (

) and (

) we can compute the induced metric on

by

\displaystyle(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=(g_{0})_{ij}+\lambda\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\cos\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}+\lambda^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\sin\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)4\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}(g_{0})_{ij}

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\cos\left(\frac{2u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\frac{3}{4}F_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}(g_{0})_{ij}+\lambda^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{A}\neq\mathbf{B},\pm}\cos\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}}{\lambda}\right)(F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}+\lambda^{2}(h_{\lambda})_{ij}.

Therefore,
g_{\lambda}
given by (
3.11
) is the induced metric if and only if the following holds

\displaystyle(F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[1]}_{u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}}\left(\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right)_{ij}+4\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(g_{0})_{ij}.

(3.41)

Since
(F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}
can be computed from (

) and (

)-(

) and
\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
from (

) is an equation for
\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
. The following lemma shows how one can solve it.

Lemma 3.3
.

If we define

\displaystyle\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=(F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}-4\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(g_{0})_{ij},

(3.42)

then (
3.41
) holds.

Proof.

If
\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
is defined by (
3.42
), then (
3.41
) rewrites
\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[1]}_{u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}}\left(\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right)=\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
. Therefore, thanks to Lemma
3.1
, proving the lemma is equivalent to

\displaystyle(\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}}-\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle=0,

(3.43)

with
\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
defined by (
3.42
). From (
3.42
) we have

\displaystyle(\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}}-\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle=\mp\frac{(\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}}-\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}}{2\left(|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}-|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}\right)}+8\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}.

From (
2.33
), (
2.17
) and (
3.19
)-(
3.20
) we find on
\Sigma_{0}

\displaystyle(\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}}-\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}-|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}}{2|\nabla_{g_{0}}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})|_{g_{0}}^{2}}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})_{\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}}^{k}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}k}

\displaystyle\quad\pm\frac{\left|\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}}\right|_{g_{0}}}{4|\nabla_{g_{0}}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})|_{g_{0}}^{2}}\Big(2|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}\pm|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\pm 3|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}+2|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-2|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}^{3}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}} -2|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}^{3}

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt\mp 4|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}} \Big).

Using now (
3.14
) we finally obtain (
3.43
) and thus the lemma.
∎

3.2.2
Initial data for derivatives of the metric and the second fundamental form

In terms of initial data for the system (
2.55
)-(
2.59
) it only remains to define the initial data for
\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{\alpha\beta}
, where
\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}
is the future-directed unit normal to
\Sigma_{0}
for
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}
. As usual when solving the Einstein vacuum equations in wave coordinates,
\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{ij}
will set the second fundamental form and
\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{0\alpha}
will ensure that (
2.67
) holds on
\Sigma_{0}
. In order to ensure that the second fundamental form is given by
k_{\lambda}
from Proposition
3.1
, we will also make a particular choice of
\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
. We first need to compute
\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}
.

The future-directed unit normal and the second fundamental form.

Using the fact that
\partial_{t}
is the future-directed unit normal to
\Sigma_{0}
for
\mathbf{g}_{0}
and using also (
3.20
), (
3.30
) and (
3.40
) we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}+\lambda^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\left(\sin\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\mathbf{T}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}+\cos\left(\frac{2u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\mathbf{T}^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)

(3.44)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{A}\neq\mathbf{B},\pm}\cos\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}}{\lambda}\right)\mathbf{T}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\lambda^{3}\mathfrak{T}_{\lambda},

where the vector fields
\mathbf{T}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
\mathbf{T}^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
\mathbf{T}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
are tangent to
\Sigma_{0}
and satisfy

\displaystyle g_{0}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}},\partial_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=-(F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{0i},

(3.45)

\displaystyle g_{0}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}},\partial_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=-(F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{0i},

(3.46)

\displaystyle g_{0}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},\partial_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=-(F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{0i},

(3.47)

and where the vector field
\mathfrak{T}_{\lambda}=\mathfrak{T}_{\lambda}^{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{\lambda}^{\alpha}=\left\{\left(F^{(2,1)}+F^{(2,2)}+(F^{(1)})^{2}\right)\left((\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{-1})^{(\geq 1)}+\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{-1}\left(F^{(2,1)}+F^{(2,2)}+(F^{(1)})^{2}\right)\right)\right\}^{\mathrm{osc}}.

(3.48)

We can now compute the second fundamental form of
\Sigma_{0}
with respect to the spacetime metric
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}
, using in particular (
1.24
), (
3.19
), (
3.31
), (
3.35
) and (
3.45
)-(
3.48
):

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda})_{ij}

\displaystyle=(k_{0})_{ij}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\sin\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}

(3.49)

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\cos\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\Big(\partial_{t}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}+4|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}(g_{0})_{ij}-\partial_{(i}u_{\mathbf{A}}(F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{0j)}\Big)

\displaystyle+\lambda\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\sin\left(\frac{2u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\left(\frac{3}{4}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}F_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}(g_{0})_{ij}-\partial_{(i}u_{\mathbf{A}}(F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{0j)}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{A}\neq\mathbf{B},\pm}\sin\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}}{\lambda}\right)\bigg(\left(|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\pm|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}\right)(F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{ij}

\displaystyle-\partial_{(i}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})(F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{0j)}\bigg)

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{ij}+\lambda^{2}\tilde{k}^{evol}_{\lambda},

where the remainder is of the form

\displaystyle\tilde{k}^{evol}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\left\{\left(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}\partial\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}\partial\left(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}-\lambda^{2}\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}\right)\right)^{(\geq 2)}\right\}^{\mathrm{osc}},

(3.50)

where by
\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}
we denote a component of the vector field
\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}
in coordinates. Note that the only terms involved in (
3.50
) that have not yet been defined are time derivatives of
\mathfrak{F}
. They can be deduced from the transport equation (
2.58
) we want
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
to satisfy in the spacetime. Thanks to (
3.21
) and (
3.30
), (
2.58
) becomes on
\Sigma_{0}

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\mu\nu|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}\Pi_{\leq}\left((\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{L_{\mathbf{A}}L_{\mathbf{A}}|_{\Sigma_{0}}}\right)(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\mu\nu|_{\Sigma_{0}}}.

Therefore,
k_{\lambda}
given by (
3.12
) is the second fundamental form if and only if the expressions (
3.12
) and (
3.49
) coincide, that is if and only if the following four identities hold:

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}_{u_{\mathbf{A}}}\left(\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)_{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{(N_{\mathbf{A}}\tilde{\otimes}\widecheck{X}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}

(3.51)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(4|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}(g_{0})_{ij}-\partial_{(i}u_{\mathbf{A}}(F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{0j)}+\partial_{t}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}\right),

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}_{u_{\mathbf{A}}}\left(\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)_{ij}-\frac{3}{2}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}F_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}(N_{\mathbf{A}}\tilde{\otimes}N_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}=\frac{3}{4}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}F_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}(g_{0})_{ij}-\partial_{(i}u_{\mathbf{A}}(F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{0j)},

(3.52)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}_{u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}}

\displaystyle\left(\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right)_{ij}-\frac{(N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\otimes}\widecheck{X}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{ij}}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}\left(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}\right)|_{g_{0}}}

(3.53)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left((|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\pm|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}})(F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}-\partial_{(i}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})(F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{0j)}\right),

and

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{ij})_{|_{\Sigma_{0}}}+(\tilde{k}^{evol}_{\lambda})_{ij}

\displaystyle=(\tilde{k}^{cons}_{\lambda})_{ij}.

(3.54)

Adjusting the second fundamental form.

First, note that (
3.54
) defines the initial data for
\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{ij}
:

\displaystyle(\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{ij})_{|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-2(\tilde{k}^{cons}_{\lambda})_{ij}+2(\tilde{k}^{evol}_{\lambda})_{ij}.

(3.55)

Second, all the terms in (
3.51
) can be computed from (
3.13
), (
3.15
), (
3.24
), (
3.33
) and (
3.34
). All the terms in (
3.52
) can be computed from (
3.33
) and (
3.34
). All the terms in (
3.53
) can be computed from (
2.68
), (
3.19
), (
3.20
) and (
3.16
). Therefore, (
3.51
)-(
3.53
) are equations for
\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
.

Lemma 3.4
.

If we define

\displaystyle(\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-\frac{(N_{\mathbf{A}}\tilde{\otimes}\widecheck{X}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}-\frac{|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}{2}\left(4\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}(g_{0})_{ij}-(N_{\mathbf{A}})_{(i}(F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{0j)}\right),

(3.56)

then (
3.51
) holds.

Proof.

If
\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
is defined by (
3.56
), then (
3.51
) rewrites
\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}_{u_{\mathbf{A}}}\left(\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)=\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
. Therefore, thanks to Lemma
3.1
, proving the lemma is equivalent to

\displaystyle(\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}N_{\mathbf{A}}}-\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle(\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}}

\displaystyle=0,

with
\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
defined by (
3.56
). Thanks to (
3.13
), (
3.15
), (
3.25
) and (
3.26
) we first have

\displaystyle(\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}N_{\mathbf{A}}}-\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}(\widecheck{X}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}}+\frac{1}{2}g_{0}^{ij}\partial_{t}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}+4|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}\partial^{i}\partial^{j}u_{\mathbf{A}}-\frac{1}{4}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}N_{\mathbf{A}}g_{0}^{ij}-\frac{1}{2}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}(k_{0})^{ij}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}\left(-\partial^{i}\partial^{j}u_{\mathbf{A}}+|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}k_{0}^{ij}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\ell}u_{\mathbf{A}}\partial^{\ell}g_{0}^{ij}\right)

\displaystyle=0.

Using now (
3.13
), (
3.27
), (
3.34
) and (
3.28
) we have

\displaystyle(\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}(\widecheck{X}^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{e}-\frac{1}{2}N_{\mathbf{A}}^{i}(e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}})^{j}\partial_{t}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}+\frac{|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}{2}(F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{0e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}g_{0}^{k\ell}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ke^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}}\bigg(-\partial^{b}(g_{0})_{\ell b}+\frac{1}{2}g_{0}^{ab}\partial_{\ell}(g_{0})_{ab}+\partial_{t}(\mathbf{g}_{0})_{0\ell}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-N_{\mathbf{A}}(N_{\mathbf{A}})_{\ell}+\frac{1}{2}N_{\mathbf{A}}^{i}N_{\mathbf{A}}^{j}\partial_{\ell}(g_{0})_{ji}+\frac{1}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}\partial_{\ell}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\bigg).

We then use (
1.23
) and (
1.26
) to obtain

\displaystyle(\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}}(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}}N_{\mathbf{A}}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}

\displaystyle=0.

This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.5
.

If we define

\displaystyle(\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{ij}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\frac{3}{2}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}F_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}(N_{\mathbf{A}})_{i}(N_{\mathbf{A}})_{j}

(3.57)

then (
3.52
) holds.

Proof.

Thanks to (
3.37
) and (
3.38
), the equation (
3.52
) rewrites

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}_{u_{\mathbf{A}}}\left(\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{2}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}F_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}(N_{\mathbf{A}})_{i}(N_{\mathbf{A}})_{j}.

Therefore, Lemma
3.1
implies that (
3.57
) is a solution if and only if it satisfies

\displaystyle(\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}N_{\mathbf{A}}}-\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle(\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{N_{\mathbf{A}}e^{(\mathbf{i})}_{\mathbf{A}}}

\displaystyle=0,

which is obviously the case.
∎

Lemma 3.6
.

If we define

\displaystyle(\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{ij}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\frac{-(|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\pm|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}})(\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{ij|_{\Sigma_{0}}}+\partial_{(i}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})(\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{0j)}}{2\mathbf{g}_{0}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}),\mathrm{d}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}))}

(3.58)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\otimes}\widecheck{X}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{ij}}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}\left(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}\right)|_{g_{0}}},

then (
3.53
) holds.

Proof.

Since
F^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
is defined by (
2.68
), the equation (
3.53
) with
\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
defined by (
3.58
) rewrites
\bar{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}_{u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}}\left(\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right)=\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and, thanks to Lemma
3.1
, holds if and only if

\displaystyle(\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}}-\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle=0,

(3.59)

\displaystyle(\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}Z}

\displaystyle=0,

(3.60)

for any vector field
Z
such that
g_{0}\left(N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},Z\right)=0
. We start with the first identity. We have

\displaystyle(\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle=\mp\frac{|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\pm|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}}{4\mathbf{g}_{0}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{A}},\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{B}})}\left((\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}}-\mathrm{tr}_{g_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right)+\frac{(\widecheck{X}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}}}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}\left(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}\right)|_{g_{0}}}

\displaystyle=-4(|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\pm|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}})\widecheck{\varphi}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\frac{(\widecheck{X}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}}}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}\left(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}\right)|_{g_{0}}},

where we used (
3.43
) and (
3.42
). Using now (
3.14
) and (
3.16
) we obtain (
3.59
). We now turn to (
3.60
). Let
Z
such that
g_{0}\left(N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},Z\right)=0
, we have

\displaystyle(\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}Z}

\displaystyle=\frac{-(|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\pm|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}})(\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}Z}+|\nabla_{g_{0}}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})|_{g_{0}}(\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{0Z}}{\pm 4\mathbf{g}_{0}^{-1}(\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{A}},\mathrm{d}u_{\mathbf{B}})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}\left(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}}\right)|_{g_{0}}}(\widecheck{X}^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{Z}.

From (
2.33
), (
2.17
) and (
3.19
)-(
3.20
) we find on
\Sigma_{0}

\displaystyle-(|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}

\displaystyle\pm|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}})(\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{N^{(\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}Z}+|\nabla_{g_{0}}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})|_{g_{0}}(\mathcal{I}^{(0,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})_{0Z}

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})^{\ell}_{\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})_{\ell Z}}{2|\nabla_{g_{0}}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})|_{g_{0}}}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}\Big(|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}-|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\pm\frac{(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}})^{\ell}_{\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\ell Z}}{2|\nabla_{g_{0}}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})|_{g_{0}}}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}\Big(|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}-|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4}\left|\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}}\right|_{g_{0}}\frac{|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot Z|_{g_{0}}}{|\nabla_{g_{0}}(u_{\mathbf{A}}\pm u_{\mathbf{B}})|_{g_{0}}}\Big(\mp|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}+|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\times\Big(|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}-|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}|_{g_{0}}|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}|_{g_{0}}\Big),

where we also used
|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{B}}\cdot Z|_{g_{0}}=\mp|\nabla_{g_{0}}u_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot Z|_{g_{0}}
. Using (
3.16
) we finally obtain (
3.60
) and thus the lemma.
∎

Setting the gauge.

We conclude this section by defining initial data for
\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{0\alpha}
. They will be chosen so that (
2.67
) holds on
\Sigma_{0}
. Thanks to the definition of
\Upsilon^{\rho}
in (
2.23
) this condition rewrites

\displaystyle\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\mu\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\sigma},

(3.61)

where we defined

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\mu\nu}\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\sin\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\left(\partial_{\mu}(\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}(\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle-\lambda\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\mu\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathcal{N}\\
T\in\{\cos,\sin\}\end{subarray}}T\left(\frac{u}{\lambda}\right)\left(\partial_{\mu}(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T})_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,h)}_{u,T})_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle-\lambda\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\mu\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathcal{I}\\
T\in\{\cos,\sin\}\end{subarray}}T\left(\frac{v}{\lambda}\right)\left(\partial_{\mu}(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T})_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T})_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\mu\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathcal{I}\\
T\in\{\cos,\sin\}\end{subarray}}T^{\prime}\left(\frac{v}{\lambda}\right)\left(\partial_{\mu}v(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T})_{\sigma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\sigma}v(\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T})_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle+(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda})_{\rho\sigma}\mathbf{g}_{0}^{\rho\gamma}\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}^{\mu\nu}\Bigg(\partial_{\mu}(\mathbf{g}_{0})_{\gamma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\gamma}(\mathbf{g}_{0})_{\mu\nu}-\sum_{\mathbf{A}}\sin\left(\frac{u_{\mathbf{A}}}{\lambda}\right)\left(\partial_{\mu}u_{\mathbf{A}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\gamma\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\gamma}u_{\mathbf{A}}(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\mu\nu}\right)\Bigg).

Note that
\tilde{\Upsilon}^{\rho}_{|_{\Sigma_{0}}}
can be completely computed. Moreover, (
3.61
) can be ensured by the following choice of initial data for
\partial_{t}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{\alpha 0}
:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{00|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\frac{2}{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{00}}\left(\tilde{\Upsilon}_{0}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{0i}\partial_{i}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{00}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{ij}\left(\partial_{i}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{0j}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{ij}\right)\right),

(3.62)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{k0|_{\Sigma_{0}}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{00}}\bigg(\tilde{\Upsilon}_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{00}\partial_{k}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{00}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{0i}\left(\partial_{t}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{ki}-\partial_{k}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{0i}+\partial_{i}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{k0}\right)

(3.63)

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{ij}\left(\partial_{i}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{kj}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{k}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{ij}\right)\bigg).

Note that the RHS of (
3.62
) and (
3.63
) are fully known on
\Sigma_{0}
and that we used the fact that
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{00}\neq 0
. Note also that the initial data for
\partial_{t}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{\alpha 0|_{\Sigma_{0}}}
allows us easily to obtain
\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{\alpha 0|_{\Sigma_{0}}}
.

3.2.3
Conclusion

Combining the results of the previous sections, we can prove the following.

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
\left(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)_{\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{A}}
be a seed. There exists a choice of initial data for the system (
2.55
)-(
2.59
) such that

(i)

the algebraic conditions (
2.60
)-(
2.67
) hold on
\Sigma_{0}
,

(ii)

the induced metric and the second fundamental form computed from
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda|_{\Sigma_{0}}}
solve the constraint equations on
\Sigma_{0}
.

Moreover, the initial data for
F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
are supported in
B_{R}
and there exists
C_{\mathrm{init}}=C_{\mathrm{init}}(N,\delta,R)>0
such that

\displaystyle\left\|F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{H^{N}(\Sigma_{0})}+\left\|F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{H^{N-1}(\Sigma_{0})}+\left\|F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{H^{N-1}(\Sigma_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{init}}\varepsilon,

(3.64)

\displaystyle\max_{r\in\llbracket 0,4\rrbracket}\lambda^{r}\left\|\partial\nabla^{r}\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+r+1}(\Sigma_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{init}}\varepsilon.

(3.65)

Proof.

We start by defining initial data for
F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
following (
3.19
)-(
3.20
) and (
3.30
). Thanks to (
3.28
)-(
3.29
), we can then define initial data for
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
following (
3.31
)-(
3.38
). This then allows us to define every terms on the RHS of (
3.42
), (
3.56
), (
3.57
) and (
3.58
) and thus allows to define
\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
,
\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
by (
3.42
), (
3.56
), (
3.57
) and (
3.58
) respectively. Note that these tensors only depend on the seed
\left(\bar{F}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right)_{\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{A}}
and are all of the form
\left\{(\bar{F}^{(1)})^{2}\right\}
, which thanks to (
3.4
) implies

\displaystyle\left\|\gamma^{(2,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right\|_{H^{N}}+\left\|\kappa^{(1,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{H^{N}}+\left\|\kappa^{(1,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{H^{N}}+\left\|\kappa^{(1,\pm)}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right\|_{H^{N}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}.

(3.66)

In particular, if
\varepsilon
is small enough then (
3.10
) holds and we can apply Proposition
3.1
and obtain a solution of the constraint equations
(g_{\lambda},k_{\lambda})
given by (
3.11
)-(
3.12
). This allows us to define every terms on the RHS of (
3.39
)-(
3.40
), (
3.55
) and (
3.62
)-(
3.63
), which in turn define initial data for
\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}
and
\mathbf{T}_{\lambda}\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}
. This concludes the definition of initial data for the system (
2.55
)-(
2.59
). Moreover, thanks to Lemmas
3.3
,
3.4
,
3.5
and
3.6
, the induced metric and the second fundamental form computed from
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}
on
\Sigma_{0}
are given by
(g_{\lambda},k_{\lambda})
and thus are solving the constraint equations on
\Sigma_{0}
. We now verify that the algebraic conditions (
2.60
)-(
2.67
) hold on
\Sigma_{0}
: (
2.60
)-(
2.61
) and (
2.66
) hold thanks to (
3.19
)-(
3.20
) and (
3.3
), (
2.62
) holds thanks to (
3.30
), (
2.63
)-(
2.64
) hold thanks to Lemma
3.2
, (
2.65
) already holds thanks to Remark
2.5
, (
2.67
) holds thanks to the choice of initial data for
\partial_{t}(\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda})_{0\alpha|_{\Sigma_{0}}}
. Finally, the estimate (
3.64
) follows from (
3.4
), (
3.19
)-(
3.20
), (
3.31
)-(
3.38
) and the estimate (
3.65
) follows in addition from (
3.17
)-(
3.18
), (
3.39
)-(
3.40
), (
3.55
) and (
3.62
)-(
3.63
).
∎

4
Solving the system

In this section, we present our result on well-posedness for the system (
2.55
)-(
2.59
) with the initial data constructed in Proposition
3.2
. It is contained in the following proposition.

Proposition 4.1
.

If
\varepsilon
is small enough and
\lambda\leq\varepsilon
, then there exists a unique solution

\left(F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}},F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}},F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}},\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}},\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}\right)

to the system (
2.55
)-(
2.59
) on
[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
with the initial data of Proposition
3.2
. Moreover, the tensors
F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
are supported in
J^{+}_{0}(B_{R})
and there exists
C=C(N,\delta,R)>0
such that

\displaystyle\left\|F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{H^{N}}+\left\|F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{H^{N-2}}+\left\|F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{H^{N-2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon,

(4.1)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\max_{r\in\llbracket 1,6\rrbracket}\lambda^{r-1}\left\|\nabla^{r}\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\max_{r\in\llbracket 0,5\rrbracket}\lambda^{r}\left\|\partial_{t}\nabla^{r}\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\max_{r\in\llbracket 0,4\rrbracket}\lambda^{r+1}\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}\nabla^{r}\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon,

(4.2)

\displaystyle\max_{r\in\llbracket 0,4\rrbracket}\lambda^{r}\left\|\nabla^{r}\tilde{\Box}_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon,

(4.3)

\displaystyle\max_{r\in\llbracket 0,4\rrbracket}\lambda^{r}\left(\left\|\partial_{t}\nabla^{r}\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+1+r}}+\left\|\nabla\nabla^{r}\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+1+r}}\right)+\max_{r\in\llbracket 0,3\rrbracket}\lambda^{r+1}\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}\nabla^{r}\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+2+r}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon.

(4.4)

The proof of Proposition
4.1
follows exactly the same lines as the corresponding statements in the singlephase case of
[
Tou23a
]
, namely Theorems 6.1 and 7.1 there. Indeed, the reasons why well-posedness of the system (
2.55
)-(
2.59
) does not follow from simple arguments are not linked with the directions of oscillations and thus don’t differ from the singlephase to the the multiphase case. Therefore, we won’t reproduce the argument and refer the reader to Sections 6 and 7 of
[
Tou23a
]
. We will however provide an outline of what is done there. As in the singlephase case, proving well-posedness is done in two steps: we first consider the equations (
2.55
)-(
2.57
) and then the equations (
2.58
)-(
2.59
).

Solving for
F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
.

The equations (
2.55
)-(
2.57
) form a triangular system for the unknowns
F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
. Thanks to (
2.32
) and (
2.37
)-(
2.38
) the equations (
2.55
)-(
2.57
) rewrite schematically

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\partial^{\leq 2}F^{(1)}+(F^{(1)})^{2}+(F^{(1)})^{3}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=\left\{F^{(1)}\partial F^{(1)}+F^{(2,1)}F^{(1)}+(F^{(1)})^{2}\right\}.

Since the transport operator
\mathscr{L}_{\mathbf{A}}
only depends on the background spacetime, a combination of the characteristics method and the energy estimate for the vector field
L_{\mathbf{A}}
easily implies the existence and uniqueness of solutions
F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
to these equations on
[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. The estimate (
4.1
) follows from the following consideration: it holds on
\Sigma_{0}
(see Proposition
3.2
) and is easily propagated for
F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
, for
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
we lose two derivatives because of the
\partial^{\leq 2}F^{(1)}
on the RHS, and because of the
F^{(2,1)}
on the RHS of the equation for
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
it lives at the same level of regularity. The only harmless difference with the singlephase case is that there is as many equations as number of directions of oscillation, and since we don’t keep track on how they mix in the equations (meaning for instance that one could see a
F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{B}}
in the equation for
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
with
\mathbf{A}\neq\mathbf{B}
) the estimate (
4.1
) is far from being uniform in
|\mathcal{A}|
.

Solving for
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}
.

Once
F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
are defined, the equations (
2.58
)-(
2.59
) form an independent coupled system for
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}
. Besides the fact that there is a transport equation for each
\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{A}
, the only difference between the multiphase and singlephase case is the presence of
\tilde{\Box}_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T}
on the RHS of (
2.59
). Indeed this term is not present in the singlephase case since its purpose is precisely to deal with the mixed harmonics in
R^{(1)}_{\alpha\beta}
. However, this new term dos not change the structure of the system. Indeed, thanks to (
2.69
) and (
2.40
),
\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T}
contains terms depending on
F^{(1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
,
F^{(2,1)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and
F^{(2,2)}_{\mathbf{A}}
and a linear term in
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
, the latter being the only one of interest at this stage. Since the term
\tilde{\Box}_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\mathfrak{g}^{(3,e)}_{v,T}
comes with an extra
\lambda
power in (
2.59
) this new term is at least better than the
\tilde{\Box}_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
in (
2.59
) and already present in the singlephase case. In conclusion, one can say that the argument (which we will briefly outline below) of Section 7 of
[
Tou23a
]
applies also here without any change.

The major issue with the coupled system (
2.58
)-(
2.59
) is the
a priori
loss of one derivative, which prevents a straightforward proof of well-posedness. Indeed, from an energy estimate on (
2.58
) one concludes that
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
lives at the same level of regularity as
\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}
, and from an energy estimate on (
2.59
) one concludes that one derivative of
\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}
lives at the same level of regularity as two derivatives of
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
(because of
\tilde{\Box}_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{A}}
on the RHS). The system (
2.58
)-(
2.59
) is thus
a priori
ill-posed. The operators
\Pi_{\leq}
and
\Pi_{\geq}
have been introduced precisely to solve this problem. Indeed, applying the Bernstein estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\Pi_{\leq}(u)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda}\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}}

(4.5)

to (
2.58
